## /products/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：products-mobile.png、products-desktop.png。
- 当前H1：Custom parts.Made to your drawing.
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Start with the shape of your part → h3: Housings & covers → h3: Shafts, fittings & pins → h3: Blocks, brackets & circular forms → h3: Impellers → h3: Plastic parts → h3: Automotive prototypes → h2: Read the setup behind the features → h2: Turn a reference into a clear scope → h3: Discuss your part
- 当前独有正文：Home / Custom parts Custom parts. Made to your drawing. Find a similar part form or see the workshop setups behind a machining question. Your controlled drawing defines the new order. Find a similar form See machining setups Machined part forms · AI-generated illustration Start with the shape of your part 35 form references, with materials and acceptance specified for your order. These are custom-part examples rather than stock configurations. Housings & covers Cavities, walls, sealing faces and mounting features. Shafts, fittings & pins Diameters, threads, flange faces and engagement. Blocks, brackets & circular forms Locating faces, bore patterns and assembly interfaces. Impellers Blade profiles, hub geometry and machining access. Plastic parts Grade, working surfaces and measurement state. Automotive prototypes Part, CAD and assembly references with scope to confirm. Plan a batch — define part numbers, revisions, quantities and release requirements. Original workshop photograph · a turned component in a subsequent milling setup. Read the setup behind the features Original photographs show the workpiece, tooling and holding relationships. Each study connects those observations to a new drawing. Turning followed by milling Locate off-axis features from the turned datum. Two milling setups Retain feature relationships after repositioning. Display housing machining Define support, flatness and finished panel interfaces. Explore all six process studies and two collections Turn a reference into a clear scope Geometry & fit Current model and controlled drawing revision, units, mating interfaces, datums and critical dimensions. Material & final condition Exact grade, condition and finish, with surfaces and dimensions to protect during treatment. Quantity & acceptance Order quantity, measurement and record requirements, destination and requested date. Minimum order from 1 piece. Production is 7–14 business days after drawing and order confirmation, including finishing. Shipping time is additional; the quotation confirms the schedule. RFQ checklist · Ordering guide Discuss your part Prepare RFQ email Prepare a draft, then attach drawings and send it from your email app.

## /products/machining-studies/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：studies-mobile.png、studies-desktop.png。
- 当前H1：Read the setup.Define your drawing.
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Choose the machining question → h3: Five-axis machining for multiple faces → h3: Turning followed by milling → h3: Two milling setups for one part → h3: Milling a pocketed enclosure component → h3: CNC milling for prismatic parts → h3: CNC machining a 29-inch display housing → h2: Separate material and form collections → h3: Plastic part forms → h3: Metal part forms → h2: Bring the question to your drawing
- 当前独有正文：Home / Custom parts / Machining studies Read the setup. Define your drawing. Six studies connect original workshop photographs to a machining question. Two separate material collections show part forms and clips, with grade and acceptance still defined for a new order. Choose the machining question These are observations and planning inputs, rather than customer performance results. Original workshop photograph Five-axis machining for multiple faces Access to angled faces and side openings. Read the machining study Inspection focus Feature positions and profiles from common datums. Original workshop photograph Turning followed by milling Off-axis features located from the turned body. Read the machining study Inspection focus Diameters, runout and milled-feature positions. Original workshop photograph Two milling setups for one part Access to different faces in two orientations. Read the machining study Inspection focus Dimensional relationships retained between setups. Original workshop photograph Milling a pocketed enclosure component Pocket depth, walls and mounting interfaces. Read the machining study Inspection focus Depth, position, flatness and final interfaces. Original workshop photograph CNC milling for prismatic parts Faces, bores, pockets and repeated forms. Read the machining study Inspection focus Relative positions, flatness and batch sampling. Original workshop photograph CNC machining a 29-inch display housing Broad panels, recessed faces and narrow edges. 29-inch is the display designation; enclosure dimensions and machine travel are not established by it. Read the machining study Inspection focus Flatness, mounting positions and cosmetic faces. Separate material and form collections Each source group stands on its own. A collection does not establish one order, confirmed material test results or a continuous operation sequence. AI-generated plastic collection cover · navigation illustration. Plastic part forms Polymer forms, fits and working surfaces. View photographs & clips AI-generated metal collection cover · navigation illustration. Metal part forms Turned interfaces, milled cavities and handling. View photographs & clips Bring the question to your drawing Include the current drawing, exact material, quantity and the feature relationships that control fit. Agree the measurement method and required records before production. RFQ checklist Prepare RFQ email Prepare a draft, then attach drawings and send it from your email app.

- 当前独有正文：Home / Custom parts / Machining studies CNC machining a 29-inch display housing A broad display housing with recessed faces, edge details and mounting features. “29-inch” is the display designation. Actual enclosure dimensions and the machining envelope must be supplied separately. A machined display housing panel. Shows Broad housing panels with slots, mounting features and recessed faces. The paired photograph compares their internal and external faces. For your drawing Specify the actual enclosure dimensions, wall sections, mounting datums and mating edges. The 29-inch display designation is separate from the enclosure dimensions. Plan the panel around its assembly interfaces The workshop records identify this as a housing for a 29-inch display. The broad panels have slots and mounting features. The display size is separate from the enclosure dimensions, which must be specified on the drawing. Support and clamping areas Show areas that may contact the fixture and surfaces that must remain protected. Review support under broad or thin sections. Panel flatness and measurement support Specify flatness and the condition in which it is measured. Agree the support arrangement used for the acceptance check. Edges, slots and finish Locate narrow edge details from mounting datums. Mark visible faces, finish requirements and dimensions that apply after treatment. Panel support and clamping areas. AI-generated concept; not the pictured housing or an approved fixture. Geometry explanation only. It does not establish the photographed part’s dimensions, approved datums, hidden features or fixture. Support, machine and inspect the panel The panel rests across the machine table beneath the spindle, with access to the internal face from above. For a similar enclosure, we check support points and clamping areas before planning tool reach and the order of operations. Review panel support Identify the stock, support points and permitted clamping areas. Protect thin sections and surfaces that affect appearance or fit. Plan tool access across the face Review access to recesses, slots and edge features. The workshop photograph shows the panel supported across the machine table. Define the final acceptance state Agree flatness, hole positions, mating edges and cosmetic checks, with the measurement support and post-finish dimensions specified. The original workshop image shows the broad panel supported across the machine table. The video and paired face photograph provide additional views of the form. The display housing panel at the CNC machine. Paired enclosure panels showing their internal and external faces. The display housing in the workshop Original views of the full housing panel and its machining setup. 18-second original workshop clip. The recording does not replace agreed inspection records. Connect the drawing to the acceptance check Locate holes and slots from the mounting datums. Define panel flatness and mating-edge relationships on the drawing. Mark cosmetic faces and dimensions that apply after finishing, then specify the measurement records needed for assembly. Planning inputs for a similar part; these are not measured results for the pictured components. Feature Define on the drawing Agree for inspection Broad and thin panels Overall dimensions, wall thickness and support areas Flatness and measurement support agreed for the panel Slots and edge contours Slot positions, profile and common datums Method for checking position and contour Mounting and mating faces Hole pattern, face relationship, finish and masked interfaces Final dimensions after treatment; cosmetic acceptance Agree measurement method, final surface condition, support, sampling and report format before production. Use the RFQ checklist Questions for this machining task Does 29 inches describe the enclosure dimensions? No. The source identifies a housing for a 29-inch display. The actual enclosure dimensions and machining envelope must be specified separately. How should panel flatness be checked? Define the flatness requirement and agree the measurement support and condition. Include the mounting datums and any post-treatment acceptance requirements. Can the workshop video replace the inspection report? The video provides a closer view of the form and setup. Request dimensional, cosmetic and any functional-test records separately for your order. Continue the drawing review Milling a pocketed enclosure component Compare cavity and wall requirements on a smaller enclosure form. Two milling setups for one part Review locating relationships across two orientations. Lid & gasket aluminum housings Compare this form’s interfaces, source reference and supply questions. For this drawing: Working diameters, datum axis, shoulder positions, thread designation and engagement. Measurement and acceptance: Mating fits, runout, thread gauging and axial dimensions. A turned component with a flange, shoulder and threaded section. Slender turned components arranged in a row. For this drawing: Cavity depth, wall thickness, datum faces, mating interfaces and cosmetic surfaces. Alloy and finish The collection labels some parts aluminum or brass; it does not establish every exact alloy. Specify grade, condition, plating and masked dimensions. Batch identity and cleanliness Tie quantities and records to part numbers and revisions. Give measurable cleanliness criteria and agree handling and packaging for the order. - 当前模块（原序）：h2: Read the part across its machining faces → h3: Common datums → h3: Tool and fixture clearance → h3: Indexed or simultaneous motion → h2: For a similar part: setup and workholding → h3: Review the model and orientations → h3: Plan access around the fixture → h3: Check relationships across faces → h2: Connect the drawing to the acceptance check → h2: Questions for this machining task → h3: Does every multi-face part need simultaneous five-axis machining? → h3: Can I use these photographs to specify a tolerance? → h3: What should the inspection report cover? → h2: Continue the drawing review → h3: Two milling setups for one part → h3: CNC milling for prismatic parts → h3: Multi-sided machined components → h2: Review a similar component
- 当前独有正文：Home / Custom parts / Machining studies Five-axis CNC machining for multi-face parts Machining holes, side openings and contoured faces on a component held in a rotary setup. Five-axis machining in progress. Shows A component held in a rotary setup, plus separate views of machined forms with holes, pockets and contoured edges. For your drawing Identify the common datums, mating interfaces and the positional relationships needed between different faces. Read the part across its machining faces Front-face holes and a curved side opening place machining features on different faces of this component. Define their relationship to a common datum. Common datums Connect features on different faces to a clear datum scheme. Changing orientation must preserve the relationships that control assembly. Tool and fixture clearance Provide the complete part and mating envelope. Check access to side openings and contours in each planned orientation. Indexed or simultaneous motion Angled faces may use indexed positioning; continuous contours may need simultaneous motion. A photograph alone does not identify the required tool path. AI-generated geometry concept · a different form from the original photographed part. Geometry explanation only. It does not establish the photographed part’s dimensions, approved datums, hidden features or fixture. For a similar part: setup and workholding The workpiece is held in front of a rotary assembly, with spindle access from above. Tool reach and clearance around the fixture need to be checked for each face before the machining sequence is confirmed. Review the model and orientations Locate the faces and openings that need access. Include the blank size, tool reach, fixture clearance and protected surfaces. Plan access around the fixture Choose the locating method and the required tool directions. The workshop photograph shows spindle access around a rotary assembly. Check relationships across faces Define which positions and profiles are checked from the shared datums, including the measurement method and report. The workshop image shows access around a rotary assembly. It does not establish whether every feature used simultaneous five-axis motion. Parts from the five-axis machining sequence. Connect the drawing to the acceptance check Identify common datums and mating features. Specify positional tolerances, surface requirements and the dimensional records you need. LZZ reviews achievable limits and the inspection method against the drawing. Planning inputs for a similar part; these are not measured results for the pictured components. Feature Define on the drawing Agree for inspection Angled holes and side openings Common datums and positional relationships Position checks against the agreed datum system Contours and restricted access Model revision, tool clearance and protected surfaces Profile comparison and permitted tool marks Separate component views Exact material and the requirements for your own part Order-specific records; photo group is a form reference Agree measurement method, final surface condition, support, sampling and report format before production. Use the RFQ checklist Questions for this machining task Does every multi-face part need simultaneous five-axis machining? No. The model, contour and available clearance determine whether indexed positions or simultaneous motion are needed. Can I use these photographs to specify a tolerance? Specify limits on your own drawing. The photographs show the setup and geometry; achievable tolerances and the measurement method need review for the part. What should the inspection report cover? Identify the critical hole positions, profiles and mounting interfaces, their datums and the results you need recorded. Agree sampling and the report format before production. Continue the drawing review Two milling setups for one part Compare datum transfer between two separate holding orientations. CNC milling for prismatic parts Compare bores and pockets referenced to mounting faces. Multi-sided machined components Compare this form’s interfaces, source reference and supply questions. For your drawing Specify pocket depth, remaining walls, corner radii and mounting interfaces, with the faces used for location. Define the pocket together with its interfaces A block-like body combines a circular recess with mounting features. Specify pocket depth, wall thickness and mating interfaces on your drawing. Pocket depth and corner radius State the required internal geometry and any permitted radius changes. Deeper pockets and restricted corners affect tool access. Remaining wall sections Show minimum wall thickness and protected areas. Review how the part will be supported while cavities and interfaces are machined. Mating and sealing surfaces Locate holes and bores from mounting datums. Mark seal lands, cosmetic faces and dimensions that apply after treatment. Review the stock and access Identify clamping faces, pocket depth and minimum wall sections. Check the tool reach and internal radii against the model. Machine the recess and interfaces Plan pockets, bores and mounting features around the agreed datums, with access for tools and clamps. Confirm final dimensions Agree depth, wall, position and mating-surface checks, including dimensions that apply after finishing. The original photograph shows the component under the spindle. Use it to understand the machining context; the wall thickness, pocket depth and exact material come from the new drawing. Machined enclosures from the milling sequence. Connect the drawing to the acceptance check Identify datum faces and mounting-hole positions. Specify pocket depths and any sealing or mating surfaces. Agree how critical dimensions will be measured and recorded. Planning inputs for a similar part; these are not measured results for the pictured components. The tool access and internal corner geometry need review before the machining route is confirmed. Which dimensions apply after finishing? Identify the finish, protected fits and final dimensions explicitly. Agree whether depth, bore and mating-surface checks occur before or after treatment. Does the enclosure form establish leak performance? Leak testing needs its own procedure and acceptance limits. The photographs and dimensional checks do not by themselves establish sealing performance. - 当前H1：CNC turning followed by milling for cylindrical parts
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Connect the milled features to the turned geometry → h3: Radial and axial location → h3: Angular orientation → h3: Edges after secondary machining → h2: For a similar part: setup and workholding → h3: Establish the turned geometry → h3: Locate for the milling operation → h3: Inspect the combined relationships → h2: Connect the drawing to the acceptance check → h2: Questions for this machining task → h3: Why relate milled features to the turned axis? → h3: Does this study show a live-tooling operation? → h3: Which dimensions need checking after milling? → h2: Continue the drawing review → h3: Two milling setups for one part → h3: Five-axis machining for multiple faces → h3: Custom CNC-turned components → h3: Rotary & cylindrical components → h2: Review a similar component
- 当前独有正文：Home / Custom parts / Machining studies CNC turning followed by milling for cylindrical parts A turned component returns to the machine for features that require milling. Milling a part after turning. Shows Circular components with bores and additional machined details, together with a subsequent milling setup. For your drawing Relate the milled holes, slots or flats to the turned axis and the shoulder or face used for axial location. Connect the milled features to the turned geometry Circular bodies combine central bores with additional milled features. The drawing needs to locate those features relative to the turned axis. Radial and axial location A datum axis controls radial relationships; an appropriate shoulder or face establishes axial position. Show both where the assembly depends on them. Angular orientation Specify the relationship between off-axis holes, slots and flats. A circular diameter alone does not define their angular orientation. Edges after secondary machining Identify burr-sensitive intersections, threads and sealing or sliding surfaces. Set edge and final-surface acceptance on the drawing. AI-generated geometry concept · a different form from the original photographed part. Geometry explanation only. It does not establish the photographed part’s dimensions, approved datums, hidden features or fixture. For a similar part: setup and workholding A turned part is located for a subsequent milling operation. Review the milled features relative to the turned datum before selecting the holding method and machining sequence. Establish the turned geometry Define the working diameters, bore, shoulders and axial dimensions. Identify which features establish the datum axis. Locate for the milling operation Choose a holding method that relates the secondary setup to the turned features and provides access to off-axis geometry. Inspect the combined relationships Agree diameter, runout and positional checks, including how the part is located for measurement. The original workshop photograph shows a subsequent milling setup. It gives context for tool access and workholding; the sequence above describes what to review for a new drawing. Parts after turning and milling. Connect the drawing to the acceptance check Specify the datum axis and critical diameters. Define hole or slot positions and any runout requirement, then agree the report format for approval. Planning inputs for a similar part; these are not measured results for the pictured components. Feature Define on the drawing Agree for inspection Turned body Datum axis, mating diameters, shoulders and axial dimensions Diameter and runout requirements Off-axis milled features Hole or slot positions relative to the turned axis Position checks using the secondary locating datums Edges and surfaces Deburring, surface finish and acceptance state Thread, edge and final-surface checks Agree measurement method, final surface condition, support, sampling and report format before production. Use the RFQ checklist Questions for this machining task Why relate milled features to the turned axis? Their positions may control assembly with the bore, diameter or shoulder. The drawing must define those relationships so the second setup and inspection can use consistent datums. Does this study show a live-tooling operation? The source shows a turned component in a subsequent milling setup. Live tooling or a separate setup for a new part is selected after drawing review. Which dimensions need checking after milling? Mark critical diameters, runout and off-axis feature positions, plus edges or surfaces affected by the second operation. Agree the final measurement state and records. - 当前独有正文：Home / Custom parts / Machining studies / Two CNC milling setups: keeping features aligned Two CNC milling setups: keeping features aligned Two milling setups provide access to different faces of the same part. Source setup 1 · original workshop photograph of the same part. Source setup 2 · original workshop photograph of the same part. Shows Two holding orientations exposing different faces of the same part. For your drawing Identify the surfaces established first and the dimensions connecting them to features reached after repositioning. Keep the feature relationships through repositioning This component requires two milling operations. Features reached in the second setup must retain their relationship to the surfaces established in the first. Datum transfer The second setup needs a defined relationship to the first. Mark the locating faces and the dimensions linking opposite or adjacent surfaces. Clamping and access Include stock dimensions, clamping restrictions and protected surfaces. Tool reach and jaw contact need review in both orientations. Inspection after repositioning State which features must be checked together after both operations. First-setup measurements alone may not establish the final relationship. AI-generated geometry concept · a different form from the original photographed part. Geometry explanation only. It does not establish the photographed part’s dimensions, approved datums, hidden features or fixture. Plan datum transfer between the two orientations The part is held in different orientations for the two operations. Choose locating faces that retain the required relationships and leave access for clamping. Choose the initial locating faces Establish stock, support and the first features. Preserve the surfaces needed to locate the part in the next orientation. Reposition using defined datums Choose contact surfaces that retain the required relationships while keeping tools and clamps clear of the next features. Check the finished relationships Measure the dimensions connecting both setups. Agree critical points, sampling and the report format. These two source photographs document different holding orientations for the same source part. Compare the accessible faces and contact surfaces, not the apparent scale of the photographs. Connect the drawing to the acceptance check Identify common datums and the dimensional relationships that must be retained between setups. Agree inspection points and acceptance records before production. Planning inputs for a similar part; these are not measured results for the pictured components. Feature Define on the drawing Agree for inspection First setup Locating faces and initial datum features Required first-stage dimensions Second setup Datum transfer and the dimensions linking both faces Relative-position checks after repositioning Finished component Material, finish and critical dimensions Agreed measurement points, sampling and report format Agree measurement method, final surface condition, support, sampling and report format before production. Use the RFQ checklist Questions for this machining task Why use two setups? A single holding orientation may block access to another face. The source photographs show two orientations; the new part model determines the required sequence. What is datum transfer? It is the relationship between the locating features used in successive setups. Define those features so dimensions remain related after the part is repositioned. How should I specify acceptance across both setups? Mark the common datums and dimensions connecting the faces. Agree how those relationships will be measured on the finished part and which results are recorded. ## /products/part-examples/custom-automotive-performance-parts-manufacturer-and-aluminum-intercooler-prototype-supplier/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：无。
- 当前H1：Aluminum intercooler prototypes
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Define the interfaces that matter → h2: Material and final condition → h2: Confirm the supplied components → h2: Drawing and acceptance requirements → h3: Send with the drawing → h3: Agree before production → h2: Reference images → h3: Source listing photograph → h2: Continue this review → h3: Two milling setups for one part → h3: CNC milling → h3: Billet aluminum intake plenums → h2: Are pressure or functional tests included? → h3: Discuss a similar part
- 当前独有正文：Home / Custom parts / Part examples Aluminum intercooler prototypes Original reference photograph of an intercooler form with its fins and end tanks. Source listing photograph Complete source in Reference images. Intercooler prototype components made around your assembly model and installation requirements. LZZ reviews the core, end tanks, ports and brackets to define machining, joining and any customer-supplied items. A form reference for a new made-to-drawing order. The approved drawing defines dimensions, material, finish and supplied scope. Minimum order From 1 piece Production 7–14 business days after drawing and order confirmation, including finishing. Shipping time is additional; the quotation confirms the schedule. Sample reconstruction and drawing approval are separate. Prepare RFQ email Define the interfaces that matter An intercooler prototype combines machined end tanks and ports with a cooling core and welded joints. Fitment is checked against the vehicle installation. Feature Define on the drawing Agree for acceptance Core, end tanks and supplied components Show the core, end tanks, ports and brackets in one assembly model. Identify customer-supplied components. Specify the material for each component, since one alloy may not suit the whole assembly. The quotation should make clear which work covers machining and joining, and which items are supplied separately. Component materials and supplied-item scope. Installation checks and pressure testing Mark the mounting and port dimensions to check after joining for correct installation. If you request a pressure test, supply the procedure and test conditions, with acceptance criteria, so we can confirm whether it can be carried out. A leak check alone does not establish cooling effectiveness or suitability for a particular vehicle. Welded interface dimensions and separate pressure criteria. Material and final condition The listed options describe a review scope. Confirm the exact grade, condition and treatment for the part on your drawing. Material options Aluminum 6061 and 3003 options. Confirm the grade for each component. Machining scope for review CNC machining combined with TIG welding. Finish options Mirror polishing, brushing, powder coating or anodizing. Confirm compatibility with the selected substrate and protect fitted surfaces. Application requirements Intercooler prototypes; define the pressure-test conditions and vehicle packaging. These are use contexts to specify, rather than confirmed customer applications. Confirm the supplied components Identify machined parts, customer-supplied or purchased items, joining and fasteners in the assembly drawing. Define acceptance after joining or assembly. Pressure, leakage, actuation, flow, cooling and other functional tests require a specified procedure, limits and responsible party. Drawing and acceptance requirements Send with the drawing Send the assembly model showing the end tanks and port orientation relative to the core. Include mounting points and the interfaces between machined and welded parts. Identify customer-supplied components and distinguish CNC machining from TIG joining. Agree before production Mark the interfaces to check after fabrication. If you need pressure or leak testing, provide the procedure, test pressure and medium, with the acceptance criteria. State the material for each component and how you plan to validate the assembly. Cooling performance requires separate testing. Check vehicle fitment during intercooler prototype development. Agree the measurement method, final condition, sampling and report format. Photographs are visual references; they do not establish measured acceptance results. Use the RFQ checklist Reference images Complete source and independently supplied views. The controlled drawing defines a new order. Source listing photograph · Finned intercooler form with curved end surfaces. Source listing photograph Finned intercooler form with curved end surfaces. Reference photograph showing repeated machined cooling-component features. Separate form reference for repeated fins and end sections; it does not establish one order or cooling performance. Continue this review Two milling setups for one part Explore locating and machining individual components across setups. Assembly and performance checks require their own scope. CNC milling Review drawing inputs, material conditions and the available machining scope. Billet aluminum intake plenums A machined internal volume and throttle flange. An intercooler also needs core and weld-joint definitions. Are pressure or functional tests included? Specify any pressure, leakage or actuation test separately, with the assembly condition and acceptance limits. The quotation must confirm the supplied components, test availability and responsibilities. Include the assembly model, component materials, supplied-core details and the intended validation plan. Include the drawing revision, quantity and delivery destination. Discuss a similar part Prepare RFQ email Prepare a draft, then attach drawings and send it from your email app.

- 当前独有正文：Home / Custom parts / Part examples Billet aluminum engine covers Original reference photograph of a curved cover and its visible tool-path texture. Source listing photograph Complete source in Reference images. Custom billet aluminum engine covers made to your approved geometry. Include the underside, seal interfaces and adjacent components so LZZ can review clearance, machining access and the visible finish. A form reference for a new made-to-drawing order. The approved drawing defines dimensions, material, finish and supplied scope. Minimum order From 1 piece Production 7–14 business days after drawing and order confirmation, including finishing. Shipping time is additional; the quotation confirms the schedule. Sample reconstruction and drawing approval are separate. Prepare RFQ email Define the interfaces that matter The visible contour is only one part of an engine-cover drawing. The underside must fit the mating perimeter and clear nearby engine components. Feature Define on the drawing Agree for acceptance Underside clearance and seal interfaces Define the underside as well as the visible cover contour, including fastener access and clearance to adjacent parts. If the cover uses a gasket or seal, provide the interface and mating-face requirements. Mating clearances and seal-face geometry. Visible finish and masked fits Mark cosmetic faces and areas where tool-path texture is acceptable. Specify the anodized or polished finish, with an appearance reference if available. Before approving the finish, agree how masked fits and finished dimensions will be checked. Appearance and post-treatment fitted dimensions. Material and final condition The listed options describe a review scope. Confirm the exact grade, condition and treatment for the part on your drawing. Material options 6061-T6 aluminum, with 7075 as an alternative. Machining scope for review 5-axis machining from solid billet with ball-nose toolpaths. Finish options Polishing, brushing and anodizing. Confirm compatibility with the selected substrate and protect fitted surfaces. Application requirements Custom engine covers and automotive development parts. These are use contexts to specify, rather than confirmed customer applications. Drawing and acceptance requirements Send with the drawing Locate mounting holes from the assembly datums. Show wall sections and fastener access, with hood, camshaft and sensor clearances. Include gasket or seal details where applicable, and specify the finish on visible surfaces. Agree before production Specify checks for the mating face and mounting pattern, with the required clearances. Define appearance acceptance separately for the outer contour. State the alloy and temper. For prototypes and small batches, check hood and camshaft clearance, with sensor clearance included in the model. Agree the measurement method, final condition, sampling and report format. Photographs are visual references; they do not establish measured acceptance results. Use the RFQ checklist Reference images Complete source and independently supplied views. The controlled drawing defines a new order. Source listing photograph · Curved cover held in a hand, showing the surface texture. Source listing photograph Curved cover held in a hand, showing the surface texture. Continue this review Milling a pocketed enclosure component Review recessed features, mounting faces and tool access on a related component form. CNC milling Review drawing inputs, material conditions and the available machining scope. Structural & aesthetic components Exposed curves and mounting points without an engine-specific clearance envelope. How are critical tolerances confirmed? Mark the critical features and datum references on the drawing. Achievable limits and measurement methods are reviewed for the material, geometry, workholding and finish before the order is confirmed. Provide the mating envelope, alloy and temper, finish requirements and whether any seal interface is present. Include the drawing revision, quantity and delivery destination. Discuss a similar part Prepare RFQ email Prepare a draft, then attach drawings and send it from your email app.

Material and final condition The listed options describe a review scope. Confirm the exact grade, condition and treatment for the part on your drawing. Material options Material options include aluminum 6061/7075, stainless steel 304/316, brass and engineering plastics. Machining scope for review CNC machining with part-specific programming and fixtures. Finish options Define the treatment for each material and part number in the batch. Confirm compatibility with the selected substrate and protect fitted surfaces. Application requirements Medical-equipment components, automation parts and instrumentation. These are use contexts to specify, rather than confirmed customer applications. Define each part and release List part number, approved revision, grade and condition, finish, initial release quantity and requested date for each item. Identify shared interfaces and approved change authority. Agree first-article checks, later sampling, traceability and nonconforming-part handling. Forecast demand is separate from the order. Drawing and acceptance requirements Send with the drawing List the material, drawing revision and order quantity for each part number. Explain the functional requirements and identify parts that must fit together. Agree before production Agree how the first parts will be approved and how later batches will be sampled and traced. State what the first-article report should contain and which features to check during production. Include requested dates. Coordinate setup changes across the parts in a high-mix, small-batch order. Agree the measurement method, final condition, sampling and report format. Photographs are visual references; they do not establish measured acceptance results. Use the RFQ checklist Reference images Complete source and independently supplied views. The controlled drawing defines a new order. Source listing photograph · Repeated turned component forms arranged in rows. Source listing photograph Repeated turned component forms arranged in rows. Continue this review Metal part forms: shafts, fittings and housings Browse grouped metal part references and review requirements by part number. Low-volume production Review drawing inputs, material conditions and the available machining scope. Pilot-batch housing references Cast blanks, turned mating parts and shared assembly interfaces. How are critical tolerances confirmed? Mark the critical features and datum references on the drawing. Achievable limits and measurement methods are reviewed for the material, geometry, workholding and finish before the order is confirmed. Include quantities per part and release, with material choices and reporting needs. Give your requested dates so availability can be checked. Include the drawing revision, quantity and delivery destination. Discuss a similar part Prepare RFQ email Prepare a draft, then attach drawings and send it from your email app.

- 当前独有正文：Home / Custom parts / Part examples / Custom CNC Impeller Machining Custom CNC Impeller Machining Source listing photograph · Original form retained; drawing and supplied scope require confirmation. Source listing photograph Complete source in Reference images. LZZ machines custom impellers with curved blades, root transitions and central hub features to your drawing. Send the controlling model, material grade and critical requirements so we can review the machining and inspection scope. A form reference for a new made-to-drawing order. The approved drawing defines dimensions, material, finish and supplied scope. Minimum order From 1 piece Production 7–14 business days after drawing and order confirmation, including finishing. Shipping time is additional; the quotation confirms the schedule. Sample reconstruction and drawing approval are separate. Prepare RFQ email Define the interfaces that matter An impeller model defines the blade contours and root transitions around the hub. Thin sections and deep pockets also affect tool access. Feature Define on the drawing Agree for acceptance Blade profiles Use the controlling 3D model to define the curved blade surfaces. Identify the profiles that need inspection and the limits that matter to your design. Agreed profiles against the controlled model. Blade roots & tool access The transitions between blades and hub affect cutter access. Mark thin sections and any areas where a radius or thickness change needs approval. Root geometry, thin sections and permitted changes. Hub bore & datum axis Define the hub bore, mating fit and datum axis together. Identify the relationships between the hub and blade geometry on the drawing. Hub fit and relationship to the blade geometry. Complete source impeller · context for the adjacent real crop. Real crop from the source impeller photograph · hub and blade-root region. Use the controlling model for the blade profiles, roots and hub bore. Surface texture in this crop does not establish roughness, balance or inspection results. Industrial fans & impellers Define blade profiles and hub geometry in the model, with workholding surfaces and material condition on the drawing. Specify any balance, speed or efficiency tests separately. Review the application requirements Material and final condition The listed options describe a review scope. Confirm the exact grade, condition and treatment for the part on your drawing. Material options titanium, Inconel and hardened stainless steel. Confirm the grade for your part. Machining scope for review Continuous 5-axis machining for curved surfaces and deep cavities with fewer setups. Finish options Lapping, polishing and specialized coating options, subject to the part requirements. Confirm compatibility with the selected substrate and protect fitted surfaces. Application requirements Impellers and complex components for aerospace, scientific equipment and robotics. These are use contexts to specify, rather than confirmed customer applications. Drawing and acceptance requirements Send with the drawing Send the controlling model, with the datum axis and critical blade profiles identified. Mark thin sections and restricted tool access. State the material condition and tolerances for the features that matter to the assembly. Agree before production Mark the profiles that need measurement and tell us which records you need. For balance or rotating-assembly tests, agree the procedures and acceptance limits separately. Tolerances are assessed for the individual drawing; no numerical tolerance is specified for this example. Agree the measurement method, final condition, sampling and report format. Photographs are visual references; they do not establish measured acceptance results. Use the RFQ checklist Reference images Complete source and independently supplied views. The controlled drawing defines a new order. Source listing photograph · Impeller with curved blades and a central hub. Source listing photograph Impeller with curved blades and a central hub. Continue this review Five-axis machining for multiple faces Review tool access and common datums across several faces. Blade-specific inspection is agreed separately. 5-axis machining Review drawing inputs, material conditions and the available machining scope. Turbine & fluid-system components Contours combined with ports and sealing interfaces. Flow or balance tests need separate criteria. Does the machining quote include balancing? Balancing and rotating-assembly tests need a separate agreement. Provide the assembly condition, test procedure and acceptance limits so availability and responsibility can be confirmed before ordering. Supply the controlling model, material condition, critical profiles and acceptance criteria for the intended operating context. Include the drawing revision, quantity and delivery destination. ## /resources/drawings-and-ordering/compare-cnc-machining-quotations/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：compare-desktop.png、compare-mobile.png。
- 当前H1：Compare CNC machining quotations
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Compare what the price includes → h2: Resolve exceptions before awarding the order → h2: Keep the agreed scope with the order → h2: Next decision → h3: Discuss the scope of your quote
- 当前独有正文：Home / Resources / Ordering guide / Compare CNC machining quotations Compare CNC machining quotations Compare prices for the same drawing revision, model, material grade, finish, quantity, required arrival date and acceptance records. Record substitutions or geometry changes as alternatives. Put excluded finishing, inspection records and delivery beside the price before choosing a supplier. Compare what the price includes Check Compare across quotations Question to resolve Drawing and quantity Part number, revision, units and quantity per line. Is this price for the current revision and requested quantity? Material Grade, condition, substitutions and required records. Is the specified material included, or is an alternative proposed? Machining and finish Required features, treatments, masking and final dimensions. Which operations or treatments are excluded? Setup and other charges Programming, setup, tooling and special documentation where quoted separately. Which charges are one-time, recurring or dependent on a repeat order? Inspection Features checked, sampling, methods and report format. Are the requested acceptance records included? Packing and transport Surface protection, pack labels, delivery term and named place. Who pays for transport, clearance, duties and taxes? Schedule Production start conditions, dispatch and transit assumptions. Does the quoted time end at completion, dispatch or arrival? Commercial terms Currency, payment stages, quote validity and treatment of changes. Which terms need written agreement before the order? Resolve exceptions before awarding the order Ask for missing items to be confirmed in writing. Keep the original requirement, proposed exception and agreed decision together. Do not assume an omitted inspection record or finish is included. When comparing total cost, include the quoted separate charges and delivery responsibilities. Do not invent an allowance for an unquoted item: request a revised quote. An earlier sample does not automatically cover a changed drawing or material. Review inspection planning Keep the agreed scope with the order Record the accepted quotation reference and date, drawing revision, quantity, approved exceptions and delivery basis. Agree how later changes will affect price, schedule and acceptance. LZZ’s order process explains the points to confirm from drawing review through delivery. How we work Next decision RFQ checklist Drawing files, specifications and a ready-to-copy inquiry brief. Approve a machined prototype Record fit, finish, function and the approval decision. Discuss the scope of your quote Bring the drawing revision and the requirements you have defined into one inquiry. The website prepares an email draft. Review it, attach drawings and send it from your email app. Prepare RFQ email

## /resources/drawings-and-ordering/critical-features-cnc-drawing/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：无。
- 当前H1：Drawing requirements & critical features
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Connect each requirement to the part function → h2: Make the requirement measurable → h2: Geometry and the machining route → h2: Material, treatment and project scope → h2: Resolve file conflicts before release → h2: Next decision → h3: Review your drawing requirements
- 当前独有正文：Home / Resources / Machining guides Drawing requirements & critical features Define how the part fits and works, then make the requirements measurable on the controlled drawing. The model, drawing revision and acceptance checks must describe the same part. Connect each requirement to the part function Identify locating faces, mating bores, shaft seats, mounting holes, sealing interfaces, sliding surfaces and load-bearing faces that matter to your assembly. Explain the function when it helps clarify the requirement. The responsible design engineer should choose the datum scheme, tolerances and applicable drawing standard. ASME Y14.5 defines the language for stating and interpreting geometric dimensioning and tolerancing on drawings and digital models. This preparation guide does not replace that standard or an engineering review. ASME Y14.5: scope of the standard Make the requirement measurable Feature or requirement Specify on the drawing Agree for acceptance Locating faces and axes The datums and feature relationships needed by the assembly. How the part will be located for measurement. Mating bores and shafts Required sizes and limits, with applicable fit requirements. Which dimensions need recorded measurements. Hole patterns and slots Locations and orientation relative to the selected references. The method and records for the specified relationships. Sealing and cosmetic faces Surface requirements and clearly identified faces. The checks and any agreed appearance reference. Threads and edges Thread designation and requirements for burrs or edge breaks. The agreed checks for these features. Treated surfaces Coated or masked areas and whether limits apply before or after treatment. The inspection stage and treatment records required. Geometry and the machining route A turned component with additional milled features needs their relationship to the turned axis defined. A pocketed enclosure needs its recesses and mounting interfaces described. These studies show the relevant part forms and setups; they do not establish tolerances for your design. Achievable tolerances depend on material and geometry, setup and measurement method. Multi-feature machined parts · original LZZ photograph. Geometry reference; acceptance follows the drawing. Turning followed by milling Pocketed enclosure study Material, treatment and project scope Material and final surface Specify grade and condition, finish and required records. Mark masked surfaces and whether dimensions apply before or after treatment. Project requirements Include applicable standards and revisions, traceability, end use and export requirements. Resolve file conflicts before release Use consistent part numbers, units and revisions across the model and drawing. State which document controls if they differ, and resolve the conflict before production. If a feature changes, identify the revision and confirm whether the earlier quote or sample approval still applies. Send your required inspection format with the inquiry. Confirm the measurement method and available records with the supplier; do not infer inspection coverage from a machine photograph or a general capability statement. Agree dimensional, appearance and functional acceptance before machining. Confirm the effect of design or material changes before repeat production. Inspection and available records Sample approval Next decision RFQ checklist Drawing files, specifications and a ready-to-copy inquiry brief. Specify material & finish Set the grade, surface treatment and dimensions after finishing. Review your drawing requirements Bring the drawing revision and the requirements you have defined into one inquiry. The website prepares an email draft. Review it, attach drawings and send it from your email app. Prepare RFQ email

## /resources/drawings-and-ordering/machined-prototype-approval-checklist/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：无。
- 当前H1：Machined prototype approval
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Agree the evaluation before making the sample → h2: Check the sample and its records together → h2: Write down exactly what has been approved → h2: Release the batch as a separate decision → h2: Next decision → h3: Plan your sample and next batch
- 当前独有正文：Home / Resources / Ordering guide Machined prototype approval Approve the sample against the purpose agreed before machining. Record what was checked, what remains open and which revision the decision covers. Receiving a sample or accepting its appearance is not the same as approving every requirement for production. Agree the evaluation before making the sample Decide whether the sample is for assembly fit, a defined functional check, appearance or a combination. Identify who supplies mating components, fixtures and test procedures, who performs the checks and who can approve the result. If prototype material or finish differs from the intended production part, record that limitation. Prototype machining Check the sample and its records together Check Evidence to review Record the decision Identity Part number, drawing revision, sample reference and quantity. Confirm which configuration is being evaluated. Material and finish The agreed grade, condition, treatment and available records. Record approved alternatives or unresolved differences. Dimensions Results for the features and limits agreed for acceptance. Link results to the sample and measurement method. Assembly fit Checks using the agreed mating parts or fixture. Record the setup, observations and any interference. Function Results from the agreed procedure, where required. State the tested conditions and any untested requirements. Appearance and handling Cosmetic faces, edges and surface protection against the agreed criteria. Identify defects or differences with photographs and references. Open issues Any deviation, missing record or requested design change. Assign the next action before releasing production. Write down exactly what has been approved Keep a short approval record with the part and sample references, drawing revision, checks performed, supporting records, decision and actual approver. Mark the decision as approved, approved with documented conditions, or not approved. List unresolved items and whether a revised sample is required. Agree the decision with the supplier. A design change after approval may require another review; do not silently carry the earlier decision into a new revision. Release the batch as a separate decision Use the approved configuration to confirm batch quantity, inspection sampling, required records, packing and schedule. A sample result applies to the sample evaluated; agree the checks needed for subsequent parts. Reconfirm any changes in material, finish or machining route before the batch starts. How we work Next decision Plan repeat production Carry the approved revision and inspection plan into a batch. Compare quotations Check scope, exclusions and delivery terms on the same basis. Plan your sample and next batch Identify the sample configuration, evaluation purpose, checks and actual approver. The website prepares an email draft. Review it, attach drawings and send it from your email app. Prepare RFQ email

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：faq-desktop.png、faq-mobile.png。
- 当前H1：Custom CNC machining FAQs.
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Quotes & ordering → h3: What is the minimum order quantity? → h3: What is the production lead time? → h3: What should I send for a quotation? → h3: Does the website send my inquiry or upload my drawing? → h3: Which payment terms should I confirm? → h3: How are samples charged? → h3: Which after-sales terms apply to my parts? → h3: How do I report a nonconforming part? → h2: Processes & specifications → h3: Which machining services can I discuss with LZZ? → h3: How should I specify the material and finish? → h3: Can every feature hold ±0.01 mm? → h2: Inspection & delivery → h3: Which inspection documents should I request? → h3: Is LZZ ISO 9001 certified? → h3: How are packing and international shipping agreed? → h3: What do the machining studies show? → h2: Have a question about your part?
- 当前独有正文：Home / Resources / FAQ Custom CNC machining FAQs. Quick answers, with the detailed guide one step away. Quotes & ordering Processes & specifications Inspection & delivery Quotes & ordering What is the minimum order quantity? You can order from 1 piece. Request separate quotes for prototypes and repeat batches. Planning prototypes and repeat batches What is the production lead time? 7–14 business days after drawing and order confirmation, including finishing. Shipping time is additional. How to order What should I send for a quotation? Send the 2D drawing and STEP model with the revision identified. Include material, finish, quantity and destination, and mark critical tolerances, threads and acceptance requirements. Drawing and RFQ checklist Does the website send my inquiry or upload my drawing? The form prepares an email draft. Attach drawings and send it from your email app; the website does not upload files or send inquiries. Prepare an inquiry Which payment terms should I confirm? Confirm the payment method and currency in the quotation, including the amount due at each stage. Payment and order terms How are samples charged? Confirm machining, setup and freight charges, including any credit toward a later order. Sample charges to confirm Which after-sales terms apply to my parts? Agree warranty coverage, exclusions and the reporting process with the order, including responsibility for rework, replacement, return freight and refunds. After-sales terms to confirm How do I report a nonconforming part? Send the drawing revision, part or batch reference, affected quantity, photographs and measurements for review against the agreed criteria. Nonconforming-part review Processes & specifications Which machining services can I discuss with LZZ? CNC milling, turning, five-axis machining, plastics and casting finish machining. For castings, agree blank supply and acceptance. Explore the five machining services How should I specify the material and finish? Specify grade, condition, treatment, cosmetic faces, masked areas and post-finish dimensions. Material and finish guide Can every feature hold ±0.01 mm? No. ±0.01 mm is a conditional reference for selected features. Limits depend on material, geometry, setup and measurement; confirm each critical tolerance. Tolerance review and quality control Inspection & delivery Which inspection documents should I request? Agree dimensional and first-article reports, sampling, format, material traceability, finishing records and cost in the quotation. Request calibration evidence separately. Inspection and delivery documents Is LZZ ISO 9001 certified? Yes. Request the current ISO 9001 certificate, validity and scope for supplier qualification. It does not replace part-specific acceptance or industry approval. ISO 9001 certificate request How are packing and international shipping agreed? Confirm protection, transport, trade term, named place and required documents for the destination. Transit is separate from production time. Shipment planning checklist What do the machining studies show? Workshop photographs show part features and setups, not customer inspection or performance results. Browse machining studies Have a question about your part? Include the drawing revision and the requirement you need to resolve. Review the email draft, attach drawings and send it from your email app. Prepare RFQ email

## /resources/ordering-guide/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：ordering-desktop.png、ordering-mobile.png。
- 当前H1：From a quote request to an approved order.
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Prepare the quote request → h3: RFQ checklist → h2: Compare the quoted work → h3: Compare quotations → h2: Approve the sample and batch → h3: Approve a machined prototype → h3: Plan repeat production → h2: Plan packing and delivery → h3: Packing & international shipping → h2: LZZ process or technical definition? → h3: Put your requirements in one request
- 当前独有正文：Home / Resources / Ordering guide From a quote request to an approved order. Choose the decision in front of you and keep the requirements consistent from inquiry to delivery. These guides help buyer preparation; agree the terms of your LZZ order separately. Prepare the quote request Compare the quoted work Approve the sample and batch Plan packing and delivery Prepare the quote request Gather the model and controlled drawing, material, finish, quantity and destination. Mark open decisions before requesting a price. Download inquiry brief RFQ checklist Drawing files, specifications and a ready-to-copy inquiry brief. Compare the quoted work Use the same revision and acceptance requirements. Check what the price includes and resolve exceptions before placing the order. Compare quotations Check scope, exclusions and delivery terms on the same basis. Approve the sample and batch Record sample acceptance, then agree the released revision, batch quantity, inspection records and schedule. The blank acceptance worksheet records requirements to agree. It is not a completed inspection report. Download acceptance worksheet Approve a machined prototype Record fit, finish, function and the approval decision. Plan repeat production Carry the approved revision and inspection plan into a batch. Plan packing and delivery Specify protection and labels. Agree the destination, shipping basis, responsibilities and documents; allow time for transit. Packing & international shipping Agree protection, delivery responsibilities and shipment documents. LZZ process or technical definition? How we work LZZ’s drawing review, sample, payment, delivery and after-sales responsibilities. Machining guides Define the geometry, material, surface and measurable requirements. Put your requirements in one request Prepare a draft, then review it, attach drawings and send it from your email app. Prepare RFQ email

## /resources/processes-materials-finishes/cnc-process-selection/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：无。
- 当前H1：Choose the CNC machining route
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Choose by geometry → h2: Workshop setups and related studies → h2: Tolerances for critical features → h2: Next decision → h3: Discuss the requirements for your part
- 当前独有正文：Home / Resources / Machining guides Choose the CNC machining route Start with the geometry and relationships between features. Some parts combine turning and milling; the drawing determines the machining route and inspection needs. Choose by geometry Route Start with the geometry Define for review CNC turning Shafts, sleeves, discs and fittings. Datum axis, diameters, shoulders, threads and runout; identify secondary milled features. CNC milling Pockets, slots, contours and mounting faces. Datums, pocket depths, hole positions, stock and clamping surfaces. Multi-axis machining Angled faces and features approached from several directions. Common datums, angular relationships, tool access and fixture clearance. A part may need more than one process. Indexed positioning and simultaneous multi-axis motion are different operations; the route must suit the drawing. CNC turning CNC milling 5-axis machining Workshop setups and related studies These workshop photographs show turning, milling and multi-axis setups. For your part, we need the drawing to review workholding, tool access and the relationships between features. These records show geometry and setups; they do not establish a machine model, a tolerance result or simultaneous multi-axis motion. Your drawing determines the route and acceptance limits. Turning · LZZ workshop Turned parts with milled features Milling · LZZ workshop Two milling setups Multi-axis setup · LZZ workshop Access to multiple faces Tolerances for critical features Mark the tolerances and datums for critical features on the drawing. The limits we can achieve depend on geometry and material, as well as the setup, finish and measurement method. Send the model and drawing revision with the material and quantity so we can recommend a process. Complete RFQ inputs Next decision Drawing requirements & critical features Define datums, fits, working surfaces and measurable acceptance. Specify material & finish Set the grade, surface treatment and dimensions after finishing. Discuss the requirements for your part Bring the drawing revision and the requirements you have defined into one inquiry. The website prepares an email draft. Review it, attach drawings and send it from your email app. Prepare RFQ email

## /resources/processes-materials-finishes/material-and-finish-checklist/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：无。
- 当前H1：Material and finish requirements
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Choose the material specification → h2: Treatment and appearance → h2: Interfaces and dimensions after treatment → h2: Solid material, coating or covering? → h2: Material and inspection records → h2: Next decision → h3: Discuss the requirements for your part
- 当前独有正文：Home / Resources / Machining guides Material and finish requirements The material grade and surface treatment belong in the same specification as the final dimensions. Include the inspection records you need when requesting a quote, so the finished part can be checked against that specification. Choose the material specification Name the exact grade, condition and standard, with any required records or permitted substitutes. If you have not selected a material, describe the loads, temperature and chemical exposure it must withstand. Include electrical or cleanliness requirements where relevant. The brochure lists the examples below; they are not a stock list or a guarantee of suitability. Confirm the exact grade and availability for your part. Material family Existing brochure examples Information to confirm Aluminum 6061, 7075, 2024 Alloy and temper; strength, stiffness, weight, environment and finish. Stainless steel 304, 316, 17-4PH Service environment, strength, condition and heat treatment. Copper and brass Exact designation required Electrical, thermal or mechanical function and applicable material standard. Engineering plastics POM, PEEK, PPSU Temperature, load, chemical exposure, electrical needs and mating material. Other named materials Titanium, Inconel, Kovar, Invar Exact designation and intended use for review. C3600, H62 and C36000 appear in different source documents. Do not assume equivalence; state the exact designation and standard. Aluminum machining Plastic machining Stainless steel machining Copper and brass machining Treatment and appearance Deburring, polishing, blasting, anodizing, passivation or plating depend on material and order requirements. Confirm the specification before production and inspect at the agreed stage. Process and specification State treatment, applicable standard and revision, coating thickness and measurement method. For anodizing, specify alloy and color; for passivation or plating, identify the process specification. Appearance Identify cosmetic faces, color, gloss, texture and agreed visual acceptance references, including blast or polish references where needed. Interfaces and dimensions after treatment Masking and interfaces Mark threads, bores, sealing and mating faces to coat, mask or protect. Identify electrical and corrosion-protection requirements and verify the surfaces after treatment. Dimensions after treatment Specify whether acceptance limits apply before or after treatment. Include surface texture and the impact of coating on fits. Edges and handling Define burr removal, edge breaks and protection of finished surfaces during packing. Red: example treatment surface; bore: interface to specify. AI-generated illustration. Solid material, coating or covering? A solid plastic part uses the specified polymer throughout. A coated metal part has a separate surface treatment; a covered wheel or roller has a separate covering as part of the component. Specify the substrate material, treatment, surfaces to coat or mask, and whether critical dimensions apply before or after coating. The original coating photographs belong to a source collection labeled Teflon surface coating. They show round components and long pieces with repeated holes; they are distinct from parts machined from PTFE material. Round coated components · original photograph from the source collection labeled Teflon surface coating Coated long components · original photograph from the source collection labeled Teflon surface coating Material and inspection records Include material traceability and treatment records in the quotation, along with dimensional inspection and any other tests. State the test limits and sampling requirements. The documents should identify the part, drawing revision and batch. Check the certificate scope against customer or industry requirements; a material name alone does not establish compliance. Quality and order records Metal part examples Next decision Drawing requirements & critical features Define datums, fits, working surfaces and measurable acceptance. Choose a machining process Compare turning, milling and multi-axis work by part geometry. Discuss the requirements for your part Include the grade and condition, final finish and records required for acceptance. The website prepares an email draft. Review it, attach drawings and send it from your email app. Prepare RFQ email

## /resources/production-and-shipping/custom-cnc-parts-shipping-checklist/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：无。
- 当前H1：Plan the shipment of CNC parts
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Protection and identification → h2: Timing and responsibility → h2: Shipment and import documents → h2: LZZ packing archive → h2: Next decision → h3: Include shipment needs in the RFQ
- 当前独有正文：Home / Resources / Ordering guide Plan the shipment of CNC parts Include the destination and packing requirements in your quote request, and confirm who is responsible for each stage of delivery. Allow for transit time when setting the arrival date; it is additional to production. Protection and identification Part protection Mark cosmetic faces, seals, threads and fragile edges that need protection, and state cleanliness or corrosion requirements. Agree how to separate and protect the parts in cartons or wooden cases, including pack weight and handling limits. Labels Specify part number, revision, batch and quantity per pack. Match the available label and record formats to the packing list. Protective packing · AI-generated illustration Timing and responsibility Production takes 7 to 14 business days after drawing and order confirmation, including finishing. Transit time is additional. Specify required arrival and dispatch dates, address and receiving contact. The quotation should name the trade term, its version and the agreed place. Confirm who arranges and pays for transport, clearance and insurance, including duties, taxes and local delivery. EXW, FOB or DDP applies as quoted. DDP is included only when the quotation says so. Courier, air or sea arrangements depend on shipment size and destination. DHL, FedEx or UPS can be discussed when selecting a courier. LZZ packing and delivery responsibilities Shipment and import documents Confirm the packing list, commercial invoice and shipment information. Material, finish and dimensional records accompany the order only as agreed. Use consistent part, revision and order references. Agree additional procurement and customs documents before production with your importing entity or freight forwarder. Requirements depend on the destination and transaction. Order documentation LZZ packing archive These photographs record packing and shipment preparation in November 2022. Specify the protection required for your own parts and shipment. The archive does not identify the destination, carrier or trade term. These are historical preparation photographs, not a current standard pack or evidence of completed delivery. Protected machined parts · November 2022 Order packing · November 2022 Prepared shipment · November 2022 Next decision Plan repeat production Carry the approved revision and inspection plan into a batch. RFQ checklist Drawing files, specifications and a ready-to-copy inquiry brief. Include shipment needs in the RFQ Send the drawing revision, material, finish and quantity with the destination, arrival date, packing needs and required documents. The website prepares an email draft. Review it, attach drawings and send it from your email app. Prepare RFQ email

## /resources/production-and-shipping/prototype-vs-small-batch-cnc/

- 类别：business；本轮当前DOM：已读；视觉截图：无。
- 当前H1：From the first sample to repeat production
- 当前模块（原序）：h2: Prototype and repeat-batch decisions → h2: Approve the batch specification → h2: Keep a clear revision and release record → h2: Confirm the next order → h2: Next decision → h3: Request a quote for the next batch
- 当前独有正文：Home / Resources / Ordering guide From the first sample to repeat production Use the first sample to check the design before approving a specification for the batch. The economical batch size depends on the part and the setup it needs. Decide which fit, appearance and functional checks the sample needs to pass before machining. Review workholding and tool access, then agree material grade and condition, finish, acceptance criteria, price and schedule. Prototype and repeat-batch decisions Decision Prototype / first sample Repeat or small batch Drawing revision Use the sample to identify fit, function or appearance questions. Release one controlled revision for the quoted batch; agree how revisions are approved and changed features identified. Setup & route Review workholding and access while confirming the design. Confirm the setup and sequence for the released revision, critical datums, and whether the route changes with quantity. Material & finish Check the specified grade, condition and final appearance. Retain the approved specification unless changed by agreement; confirm material documents, finish standard, masking and dimensions after treatment. Inspection Define fit, function and sample acceptance checks. Identify batch sampling and critical-feature records; confirm method, instrument evidence and report format available. Price & timing Confirm the prototype price and schedule for the specific part. Requote the batch quantity and price; production takes 7-14 business days after drawing and order confirmation, including finishing. Add transit separately. Approve the batch specification The batch order should use the approved drawing, material, finish and acceptance plan. If the drawing changes, review the changed features before approving production of the revised part. Approved specification Agree revision approval, material documents, finish standard, masking and dimensions after treatment. Retain the specification unless a change is agreed. Batch inspection Agree how many parts to sample and which critical features need recorded measurements. Confirm the measurement methods, instrument documentation and available report format, along with the documents needed at delivery. Quantity and schedule Confirm the setup sequence and critical datums with us. Agree the batch quantity, price and production slot, and check whether the quantity changes the machining route. Orders start at 1 piece. For production timing and transit conditions, use the price and timing row above. Batch inspection planning Keep a clear revision and release record Stage Record before continuing Sample evaluation Sample reference, drawing revision, material and finish; checks performed and open issues. Design change Changed features, revised files and any effect on price, route, inspection or schedule. Batch release Approved revision, quantity, sampling plan, required records and confirmation of release. How we work Confirm the next order Compare quotes for the same revision, material, finish and quantity. Check setup, inspection records, packaging and delivery alongside unit price. Record exclusions and proposed substitutions; agree how changes, nonconforming parts and repeat releases will be handled. Compare CNC machining quotations Small production runs Next decision Approve a machined prototype Record fit, finish, function and the approval decision. Packing & international shipping Agree protection, delivery responsibilities and shipment documents. Request a quote for the next batch Send the controlled drawing with the first and repeat quantities, requested arrival date and required records. The website prepares an email draft. Review it, attach drawings and send it from your email app. Prepare RFQ email
